  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).

    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname

    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.

    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.

    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.

    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.

    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

QUESTION: Among the events attended by more than 10 members of the Student_Club, how many of them are meetings?
⚠️  HINT (follow these definitions EXACTLY):
  meetings events refers to type = 'Meeting'; attended by more than 10 members refers to COUNT(event_id) > 10

SIMILAR SOLVED EXAMPLES (different databases — copy the SQL *style* and output-column conventions, not the table names):

  Q: How many of the students joined two organization?
  Hint: joined two organization refers to COUNT(organ) > = 2
  SQL: SELECT COUNT(name) FROM enlist WHERE organ >= 2

Schema:
DATABASE: student_club

TABLE: attendance
  link_to_event (TEXT) [PK]
  link_to_member (TEXT) [PK]
  FK: link_to_member → member.member_id
  FK: link_to_event → event.event_id

TABLE: budget
  budget_id (TEXT) [PK]
  category (TEXT)  e.g. 'Advertisement', 'Food'
  spent (REAL)  e.g. 67.81, 121.14
  remaining (REAL)  e.g. 7.19, 28.86
  amount (INTEGER)  e.g. 75, 150
  event_status (TEXT)  e.g. 'Closed', 'Open'
  link_to_event (TEXT)  e.g. 'recI43CzsZ0Q625ma', 'recggMW2eyCYceNcy'
  FK: link_to_event → event.event_id

TABLE: event
  event_id (TEXT) [PK]

  type (TEXT)  e.g. 'Meeting', 'Election'

  location (TEXT)  e.g. 'MU 215', 'Campus Soccer/Lacrosse stadium'
  status (TEXT)  e.g. 'Open', 'Planning'

TABLE: expense

  expense_description (TEXT)  e.g. 'Post Cards, Posters', 'Water, Cookies'
  expense_date (TEXT)  e.g. '2019-08-20', '2019-10-08'
  cost (REAL)  e.g. 122.06, 20.2
  approved (TEXT)  e.g. 'true'
  link_to_member (TEXT)  e.g. 'rec4BLdZHS2Blfp4v', 'recro8T1MPMwRadVH'
  link_to_budget (TEXT)  e.g. 'recvKTAWAFKkVNnXQ', 'recy8KY5bUdzF81vv'
  FK: link_to_member → member.member_id
  FK: link_to_budget → budget.budget_id

TABLE: income
  income_id (TEXT) [PK]
  date_received (TEXT)  e.g. '2019-10-17', '2019-09-04'
  amount (INTEGER)  e.g. 50, 200
  source (TEXT)  e.g. 'Dues', 'Fundraising'
  notes (TEXT)  e.g. 'Secured donations to help p...', 'Annual funding from Student...'
  link_to_member (TEXT)  e.g. 'reccW7q1KkhSKZsea', 'recTjHY5xXhvkCdVT'
  FK: link_to_member → member.member_id

TABLE: major
  major_id (TEXT) [PK]
  major_name (TEXT)  e.g. 'Outdoor Product Design and ...', 'Agricultural Communication'
  department (TEXT)  e.g. 'School of Applied Sciences,...', 'Watershed Sciences Department'
  college (TEXT)  e.g. 'College of Agriculture and ...', 'College of Natural Resources'

TABLE: member
  member_id (TEXT) [PK]
  first_name (TEXT)  e.g. 'Angela', 'Grant'
  last_name (TEXT)  e.g. 'Sanders', 'Gilmour'
  email (TEXT)  e.g. 'angela.sanders@lpu.edu', 'grant.gilmour@lpu.edu'
  position (TEXT)  e.g. 'Member', 'Inactive'
  t_shirt_size (TEXT)  e.g. 'Medium', 'X-Large'
  phone (TEXT)  e.g. '(651) 928-4507', '403-555-1310'
  zip (INTEGER)  e.g. 55108, 29440
  link_to_major (TEXT)  e.g. 'recxK3MHQFbR9J5uO', 'rec7BxKpjJ7bNph3O'
  FK: zip → zip_code.zip_code
  FK: link_to_major → major.major_id

TABLE: zip_code
  zip_code (INTEGER) [PK]
  type (TEXT)  e.g. 'Unique', 'Standard'
  city (TEXT)  e.g. 'Holtsville', 'Adjuntas'
  county (TEXT)  e.g. 'Suffolk County', 'Adjuntas Municipio'
  state (TEXT)  e.g. 'New York', 'Puerto Rico'
  short_state (TEXT)  e.g. 'NY', 'PR'

Follow the Hint above, explore the DB if needed, test your SQL, then FINAL("your sql").

🤖 model turn:

```python
print(db.execute("""
SELECT COUNT(*)
FROM (
  SELECT e.event_id
  FROM event e
  JOIN attendance a
    ON e.event_id = a.link_to_event
  WHERE e.type = 'Meeting'
  GROUP BY e.event_id
  HAVING COUNT(a.link_to_event) > 10
) AS t;
"""))
```
FINAL("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM (SELECT e.event_id FROM event e JOIN attendance a ON e.event_id = a.link_to_event WHERE e.type = 'Meeting' GROUP BY e.event_id HAVING COUNT(e.event_id) > 10) AS t;")

⚙️ observation:

{'columns': ['COUNT(*)'], 'rows': [[4]], 'truncated': False, 'error': None}

🤖 model turn:

FINAL("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM (SELECT e.event_id FROM event e JOIN attendance a ON e.event_id = a.link_to_event WHERE e.type = 'Meeting' GROUP BY e.event_id HAVING COUNT(a.link_to_event) > 10) AS t;")

FINAL SQL: SELECT COUNT(*) FROM (SELECT e.event_id FROM event e JOIN attendance a ON e.event_id = a.link_to_event WHERE e.type = 'Meeting' GROUP BY e.event_id HAVING COUNT(a.link_to_event) > 10) AS t;

PRED result: [[4]]

GOLD SQL: SELECT T1.event_name FROM event AS T1 INNER JOIN attendance AS T2 ON T1.event_id = T2.link_to_event GROUP BY T1.event_id HAVING COUNT(T2.link_to_event) > 10 EXCEPT SELECT T1.event_name FROM event AS T1 WHERE T1.type = 'Meeting'

  'NO'

    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).

    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.

    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.

    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.

    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join

    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.

    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,

QUESTION: What is the difference in the annual average consumption of the customers with the least amount of consumption paid in CZK for 2013 between SME and LAM, LAM and KAM, and KAM and SME?
⚠️  HINT (follow these definitions EXACTLY):
  annual average consumption of customer with the lowest consumption in each segment = total consumption per year / the number of customer with lowest consumption in each segment; Difference in annual average = SME's annual average - LAM's annual average; Difference in annual average = LAM's annual average - KAM's annual average; Year 2013 can be presented as Between 201301 And 201312; The first 4 strings of the Date values in the yearmonth table can represent year.

SIMILAR SOLVED EXAMPLES (different databases — copy the SQL *style* and output-column conventions, not the table names):

  Q: How many customers are from the place with the highest average income per month?
  Hint: place with the highest average income per month refers to GEOID where MAX(INCOME_K);
  SQL: SELECT COUNT(T1.ID) FROM Customers AS T1 INNER JOIN Demog AS T2 ON T1.GEOID = T2.GEOID ORDER BY T2.INCOME_K DESC LIMIT 1

```python
print(db.execute("""
WITH cust_totals AS (
  SELECT c.Segment, y.CustomerID, SUM(y.Consumption) AS total_consumption
  FROM yearmonth AS y
  JOIN customers AS c
    ON y.CustomerID = c.CustomerID
  WHERE c.Currency = 'CZK'
    AND y.Date BETWEEN '201301' AND '201312'
  GROUP BY c.Segment, y.CustomerID
),
segment_min AS (
  SELECT Segment, MIN(total_consumption) AS min_consumption
  FROM cust_totals
  GROUP BY Segment
),
segment_avg AS (
  SELECT ct.Segment, SUM(ct.total_consumption) * 1.0 / COUNT(*) AS annual_avg
  FROM cust_totals AS ct
  JOIN segment_min AS sm
    ON ct.Segment = sm.Segment
   AND ct.total_consumption = sm.min_consumption
  GROUP BY ct.Segment
)
SELECT
  (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'SME') - (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'LAM'),
  (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'LAM') - (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'KAM'),
  (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'KAM') - (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'SME');
"""))
```
FINAL("WITH cust_totals AS ( SELECT c.Segment, y.CustomerID, SUM(y.Consumption) AS total_consumption FROM yearmonth AS y JOIN customers AS c ON y.CustomerID = c.CustomerID WHERE c.Currency = 'CZK' AND y.Date BETWEEN '201301' AND '201312' GROUP BY c.Segment, y.CustomerID ), segment_min AS ( SELECT Segment, MIN(total_consumption) AS min_consumption FROM cust_totals GROUP BY Segment ), segment_avg AS ( SELECT ct.Segment, SUM(ct.total_consumption) * 1.0 / COUNT(*) AS annual_avg FROM cust_totals AS ct JOIN segment_min AS sm ON ct.Segment = sm.Segment AND ct.total_consumption = sm.min_consumption GROUP BY ct.Segment ) SELECT (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'SME') - (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'LAM'), (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'LAM') - (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'KAM'), (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'KAM') - (SELECT annual_avg FROM segment_avg WHERE Segment = 'SME');")

```python
print(db.execute("""
WITH annual AS (
  SELECT c.Segment, y.CustomerID, SUM(y.Consumption) AS total_consumption
  FROM yearmonth AS y
  JOIN customers AS c
    ON y.CustomerID = c.CustomerID
  WHERE c.Currency = 'CZK'
    AND y.Date BETWEEN '201301' AND '201312'
  GROUP BY c.Segment, y.CustomerID
),
min_consumption AS (
  SELECT Segment, MIN(total_consumption) AS min_total
  FROM annual
  GROUP BY Segment
),
avg_min AS (
  SELECT a.Segment, SUM(a.total_consumption) * 1.0 / COUNT(*) AS avg_consumption
  FROM annual AS a
  JOIN min_consumption AS m
    ON a.Segment = m.Segment
   AND a.total_consumption = m.min_total
  GROUP BY a.Segment
)
SELECT
  (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'SME') - (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'LAM'),
  (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'LAM') - (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'KAM'),
  (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'KAM') - (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'SME');
"""))
```
FINAL("WITH annual AS ( SELECT c.Segment, y.CustomerID, SUM(y.Consumption) AS total_consumption FROM yearmonth AS y JOIN customers AS c ON y.CustomerID = c.CustomerID WHERE c.Currency = 'CZK' AND y.Date BETWEEN '201301' AND '201312' GROUP BY c.Segment, y.CustomerID ), min_consumption AS ( SELECT Segment, MIN(total_consumption) AS min_total FROM annual GROUP BY Segment ), avg_min AS ( SELECT a.Segment, SUM(a.total_consumption) * 1.0 / COUNT(*) AS avg_consumption FROM annual AS a JOIN min_consumption AS m ON a.Segment = m.Segment AND a.total_consumption = m.min_total GROUP BY a.Segment ) SELECT (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'SME') - (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'LAM'), (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'LAM') - (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'KAM'), (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'KAM') - (SELECT avg_consumption FROM avg_min WHERE Segment = 'SME');")

```python
print(db.execute("""
WITH seg AS (
  SELECT c.Segment,
         SUM(CASE WHEN SUBSTR(y.Date,1,4) = '2013' THEN y.Consumption ELSE 0 END) AS c2013,
         SUM(CASE WHEN SUBSTR(y.Date,1,4) = '2012' THEN y.Consumption ELSE 0 END) AS c2012
  FROM yearmonth y
  JOIN customers c
    ON y.CustomerID = c.CustomerID
  WHERE c.Currency = 'EUR'
    AND c.Segment IN ('SME', 'LAM', 'KAM')
  GROUP BY c.Segment
),
pct AS (
  SELECT Segment,
         (c2013 - c2012) * 100.0 / c2013 AS pct_increase
  FROM seg
)
SELECT
  (SELECT Segment FROM pct ORDER BY pct_increase DESC LIMIT 1) AS biggest_segment,
  (SELECT Segment FROM pct ORDER BY pct_increase ASC LIMIT 1) AS lowest_segment
FROM pct
LIMIT 1;
"""))

    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,

QUESTION: Is it true that more SMEs pay in Czech koruna than in euros? If so, how many more?
⚠️  HINT (follow these definitions EXACTLY):
  Amount of more SMEs = Total of SMEs pay using Currency CZK - Total of SMEs pay using Currency EUR

SIMILAR SOLVED EXAMPLES (different databases — copy the SQL *style* and output-column conventions, not the table names):

  Q: What is the percentage of the payment amount in 2004 was made by Atelier graphique?
  Hint: DIVIDE(SUM(amount) where customerName = 'Atelier graphique'), (SUM(amount)) as percentage where year(paymentDate) = 2004;
  SQL: SELECT SUM(CASE WHEN t1.customerName = 'Atelier graphique' THEN t2.amount ELSE 0 END) * 100 / SUM(t2.amount) FROM customers AS t1 INNER JOIN payments AS t2 ON t1.customerNumber = t2.customerNumber WHERE STRFTIME('%Y', t2.paymentDate) = '2004'

🤖 model turn:

```python
print(db.execute("""
SELECT
  IIF(czk_cnt > eur_cnt, 'YES', 'NO') AS more_in_czk,
  czk_cnt - eur_cnt AS amount_more
FROM (
  SELECT
    SUM(CASE WHEN Segment = 'SME' AND Currency = 'CZK' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS czk_cnt,
    SUM(CASE WHEN Segment = 'SME' AND Currency = 'EUR' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS eur_cnt
  FROM customers
);
"""))
```

    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)

WITH target_customer AS (
  SELECT CustomerID
  FROM transactions_1k
  WHERE Date = '2012-08-25'
    AND Price = 634.8

),

  SELECT
    CustomerID,
    SUM(CASE WHEN substr(Date,1,4) = '2012' THEN Consumption ELSE 0 END) AS consumption_2012,
    SUM(CASE WHEN substr(Date,1,4) = '2013' THEN Consumption ELSE 0 END) AS consumption_2013

"""))

RULES:
  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).
  • If the question asks multiple things ("What is X? Who is Y?" / "state A and B"),
    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname
  • For superlatives (oldest/highest/best/dumbest) return exactly one row:
    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.
  • When using T1/T2 aliases, double-check which table each SELECTed column belongs to
    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

WORKFLOW:

Turn 1 — READ HINT + EXPLORE (one code block):
  ① Read the Hint carefully — it defines exact column values, date formats, and
    computation formulas. Treat every definition in the Hint as ground truth.
    Examples of what Hints tell you:
      "September 2013 refers to 201309"  → WHERE date_col = '201309'  (not LIKE '2013-09%')
      "ratio = count(A) / count(B)"      → SELECT COUNT(CASE WHEN x='A' THEN 1 END)*1.0 / COUNT(CASE WHEN x='B' THEN 1 END)
      "meeting events refers to type = 'Meeting'" → WHERE type = 'Meeting'
  ② If any names, locations, or string values are NOT defined by the Hint,
    use db.sample_values() to see how they are actually stored in the database.

  ```python
  print(db.execute("YOUR SQL HERE"))
  ```

Turn 3 — FINALIZE (plain text only, never inside a code block):
  FINAL("YOUR SQL HERE")

RULES:
  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).
  • If the question asks multiple things ("What is X? Who is Y?" / "state A and B"),
    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname
  • For superlatives (oldest/highest/best/dumbest) return exactly one row:
    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.
  • When using T1/T2 aliases, double-check which table each SELECTed column belongs to
    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

🤖 model turn:

FINAL("SELECT T1.event_name FROM event AS T1 INNER JOIN attendance AS T2 ON T1.event_id = T2.link_to_event WHERE T1.type <> 'Fundraising' GROUP BY T1.event_id, T1.event_name HAVING COUNT(T1.event_id) > 20")

FINAL SQL: SELECT T1.event_name FROM event AS T1 INNER JOIN attendance AS T2 ON T1.event_id = T2.link_to_event WHERE T1.type <> 'Fundraising' GROUP BY T1.event_id, T1.event_name HAVING COUNT(T1.event_id) > 20

PRED result: [['October Speaker'], ['September Speaker'], ['Registration'], ['October Meeting'], ['November Speaker'], ['November Meeting'], ['Yearly Kickoff'], ['September Meeting']]

GOLD SQL: SELECT T1.event_name FROM event AS T1 INNER JOIN attendance AS T2 ON T1.event_id = T2.link_to_event GROUP BY T1.event_id HAVING COUNT(T2.link_to_event) > 20 EXCEPT SELECT T1.event_name FROM event AS T1 WHERE T1.type = 'Fundraiser'

GOLD result: [['November Meeting'], ['November Speaker'], ['October Meeting'], ['October Speaker'], ['Registration'], ['September Meeting'], ['September Speaker'], ['Yearly Kickoff']]

RULES:
  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).
  • If the question asks multiple things ("What is X? Who is Y?" / "state A and B"),
    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname
  • For superlatives (oldest/highest/best/dumbest) return exactly one row:
    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.

    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.

    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)

SIMILAR SOLVED EXAMPLES (different databases — copy the SQL *style* and output-column conventions, not the table names):

  Q: Calculate the difference between the total number of students and the number of international international students in Univeristy of Tokyo from 2011 to 2014.
  Hint: international students refers to DIVIDE(MULTIPLY(num_students, pct_international_students), 100); difference refers to SUBTRACT(SUM(num_students), SUM(DIVIDE(MULTIPLY(num_students, pct_international_students), 100))); in University of Tokyo refers to university_name = 'University of Tokyo'; from 2011 to 2014 refers to year BETWEEN 2011 AND 2014
  SQL: SELECT SUM(T1.num_students) - SUM(CAST(T1.num_students * T1.pct_international_students AS REAL) / 100) FROM university_year AS T1 INNER JOIN university AS T2 ON T1.university_id = T2.id WHERE T1.year BETWEEN 2011 AND 2014 AND T2.university_name = 'University of Tokyo'

    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

    Examples of what Hints tell you:
      "September 2013 refers to 201309"  → WHERE date_col = '201309'  (not LIKE '2013-09%')
      "ratio = count(A) / count(B)"      → SELECT COUNT(CASE WHEN x='A' THEN 1 END)*1.0 / COUNT(CASE WHEN x='B' THEN 1 END)

  ```

Turn 3 — FINALIZE (plain text only, never inside a code block):
  FINAL("YOUR SQL HERE")

RULES:
  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).
  • If the question asks multiple things ("What is X? Who is Y?" / "state A and B"),
    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname
  • For superlatives (oldest/highest/best/dumbest) return exactly one row:
    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.
  • When using T1/T2 aliases, double-check which table each SELECTed column belongs to
    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

  Q: How many times is the number of "Women's Clothing" Yelp businesses to "Men's Clothing"?
  Hint: "Women's Clothing" Yelp businesses refers to  category_name = 'Women''s Clothing'; "Men's Clothing" refers to category_name = 'Men''s Clothing'; times refers to DIVIDE(COUNT(category_name = 'Women''s Clothing'), COUNT(category_name = 'Men''s Clothing'))
  SQL: SELECT CAST(SUM(CASE WHEN T2.category_name = 'Women''s Clothing' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS REAL) / SUM(CASE WHEN T2.category_name = 'Men''s Clothing' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS TIMES FROM Business_Categories AS T1 INNER JOIN Categories AS T2 ON T1.category_id = T2.category_id

    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)

RULES:
  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).
  • If the question asks multiple things ("What is X? Who is Y?" / "state A and B"),
    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname
  • For superlatives (oldest/highest/best/dumbest) return exactly one row:
    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.

    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)

You are a Text-to-SQL expert with access to a live database. Use it to verify your SQL before finalizing.

AVAILABLE TOOLS (call these inside ```python blocks):
  db.execute("SQL")                        — run any SELECT and see results
  db.sample_values("table", "column")     — see actual values stored in a column

WORKFLOW:

Turn 1 — READ HINT + EXPLORE (one code block):
  ① Read the Hint carefully — it defines exact column values, date formats, and
    computation formulas. Treat every definition in the Hint as ground truth.
    Examples of what Hints tell you:
      "September 2013 refers to 201309"  → WHERE date_col = '201309'  (not LIKE '2013-09%')
      "ratio = count(A) / count(B)"      → SELECT COUNT(CASE WHEN x='A' THEN 1 END)*1.0 / COUNT(CASE WHEN x='B' THEN 1 END)
      "meeting events refers to type = 'Meeting'" → WHERE type = 'Meeting'
  ② If any names, locations, or string values are NOT defined by the Hint,
    use db.sample_values() to see how they are actually stored in the database.

Turn 2 — TEST your SQL (one code block):
  ```python
  print(db.execute("YOUR SQL HERE"))
  ```

Turn 3 — FINALIZE (plain text only, never inside a code block):
  FINAL("YOUR SQL HERE")

RULES:
  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).
  • If the question asks multiple things ("What is X? Who is Y?" / "state A and B"),
    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname
  • For superlatives (oldest/highest/best/dumbest) return exactly one row:
    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.
  • When using T1/T2 aliases, double-check which table each SELECTed column belongs to
    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.
  • When using T1/T2 aliases, double-check which table each SELECTed column belongs to
    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

  Q: How many times is the number of active apps in the event that happened at 7:50:28 on 2016/5/2 than in the event that happened at 7:41:03 on 2016/5/2?
  Hint: how many times = DIVIDE(SUM(IF(timestamp = '2016/5/2 7:50:28', 1,0)), SUM(IF(timestamp = '2016/5/2 7:41:03',1,0))); active refers to is_active = '1'; at 7:50:28 on 2016/5/2 refers to timestamp = '2016/5/2 7:50:28'; at 7:41:03 on 2016/5/2 refers to timestamp = '2016/5/2 7:41:03';
